AND 227 OF THE CONSTITUTION OF INDIA PRAYING TO PASS

RESERVED FOR ORDERS, COMING ON FOR PRONOUNCEMENT 
THIS DAY, THE COURT MADE THE FOLLOWING:- 
 
 


--- PAGE 292 ---
- 292 - 
 
 
WP No. 8442 of 2020 
AND CONNECTED MATTERS 
 
 
ORDER 
 
 
Cluster of these petitions seek to challenge an order 
dated 4-04-2018 by which the claim of the petitioners in all 
these petitions for regularization of their services is turned 
down and have sought a writ in the nature of mandamus 
directing the respondents to regularize the services of the 
applicants by declaring the appointments of the petitioners 
as valid and further seek that their services should be 
continued till their cases are considered for regularization.  
 
 
2. 
Heard 
Sri.V.Lakshminarayana, 
learned 
Senior 
Counsel for petitioners, Sri.Dhyan Chinnappa, learned 
Additional Advocate General along with Smt.M.C.Nagashree, 
learned Additional Government Advocate for the State and 
Prof. Ravivarma Kumar, learned Senior Counsel for the 
respondent- Society. 
 
 
3. Brief facts leading to the filing of the present 
petitions, as borne out from the pleadings are, as follows:- 


--- PAGE 293 ---
- 293 - 
 
 
WP No. 8442 of 2020 
AND CONNECTED MATTERS 
 
 
 
 
Till the year 1977-78 private educational institutions 
and religious mutts were running residential schools in the 
State of Karnataka.  In the year 1977 the Social Welfare 
Department started residential schools to the children of 
tribal community under the name Ashrama schools.  In 
these schools tribal children aged 5 to 10 years were given 
free education and hostel facilities including food. In the 
year 1986 Government of India started residential schools in 
every District in the country in the name and style of 
‘Navodaya Model Residential Schools’ for encouraging 
talented and meritorious children belonging to Scheduled 
Castes, Scheduled Tribes, Minorities and other Backward 
Classes residing in rural areas.  
 
4. In the year 1994-95 Government of Karnataka also 
started residential schools in rural areas to tap and 
encourage the talent and identify meritorious children by 
providing free and quality education. These residential 
schools were imparting education under different names  


--- PAGE 294 ---
- 294 - 
 
 
WP No. 8442 of 2020 
AND CONNECTED MATTERS 
 
 
 
such as Navodaya Model Residential Schools, Morarji Desai 
Model Residential Schools, Ekalavya Residential Schools, 
Kittoor Rani Chennamma Residential Schools and Minority 
Residential Schools.  These residential schools came under 
the control of the Social Welfare Department, Backward 
Class 
Department, 
Tribal 
Welfare 
Department 
and 
Education Department. In all these residential schools free 
education, boarding, lodging, medical facilities and all 
necessary concomitants for the purpose of imparting 
education were provided.  
 
5. The recruitment of teachers into these schools was 
yet to come about as the Cadre and Recruitment Rules to 
the aforesaid different residential schools in the matter of 
recruitment of teaching and non-teaching staff had not been 
notified. Therefore, the Government from time to time had 
issued Government orders, circulars and guidelines for 
appointment 
of 
teachers 
and 
non-teaching 
staff 
on 
temporary and contract basis. Owing to the purpose for  


--- PAGE 295 ---
- 295 - 
 
 
WP No. 8442 of 2020 
AND CONNECTED MATTERS 
 
 
 
which the schools were established, temporary and contract 
teachers were appointed from time to time. 
 
 
6. In order to bring these residential schools under one 
entity with regard to control, supervision and management, 
Government of Karnataka on 06-10-1999 registered a 
society 
called 
the 
Karnataka 
Residential 
Educational 
Institutions Society (‘the Society’ for short) under the 
provisions of the Karnataka Societies Registration Act, 1960.  
All the residential schools aforesaid in different names were 
brought under the Society. Bye-laws of the Society were 
framed for management of existing schools and also new 
schools that were to be set up. It is the Society which later 
starts new residential schools in addition to existing schools. 
Even in new residential schools teaching and non-teaching 
staff were appointed either on temporary or contract basis.  
 
7. 
From 
the 
academic 
year 
2004-2005 
the 
administration and management of aforesaid schools which  


--- PAGE 296 ---
- 296 - 
 
 
WP No. 8442 of 2020 
AND CONNECTED MATTERS 
 
 
 
came under the Society were handed over to Zilla 
Panchayats in the respective Districts by an order dated 5-
10-2004.  Subsequently, in the year 2006, Government of 
Karnataka issued a manual for residential schools specifying 
the procedure and guidelines for day-to-day management, 
supervision and control of these residential schools. The 
Society which was co-existing with Zilla Panchayats insofar 
as the schools were concerned, requested respective Deputy 
Commissioners and all other departments to appoint 
teaching and non-teaching staff on contract basis for all 
residential schools existing and newly established, on the 
ground that the necessity of these teachers was paramount. 
Accordingly, appointments to all these residential schools 
were made.  
 
 
8. On 21-01-2011 the Department of Social Welfare 
notified Cadre and Recruitment Regulations called the 
Karnataka Residential Educational Institutions Society 
(Cadre and Recruitment) Regulations,  2011 specifying the  


--- PAGE 297 ---
- 297 - 
 
 
WP No. 8442 of 2020 
AND CONNECTED MATTERS 
 
 
 
category of posts, method of recruitment, qualification, 
absorption, retirement and other service conditions. The 
contract employees working in these residential schools had 
placed their request for regularization of their services and 
after due consideration, the State Government enacted 
Absorption Regulations, 2011. The Absorption Regulations 
provided for absorption of Principals and Teachers appointed 
prior to the academic year 2004-05 and continued till the 
commencement of the afore-quoted Cadre and Recruitment 
Regulations as a onetime measure subject to satisfaction of 
certain conditions. A few of the Principals and large chunk of 
teachers were all absorbed under these Regulations.  
 
 
9. Several writ petitions came to be filed before this 
Court in Writ Petition Nos.20204-20364 of 2011 and 
connected cases seeking the following prayers: 
"i) 
To direct the respondents to extend the 
benefit of Karnataka Residential Educatonal 
Institutions (Absorption of Persons working 
as Principals and Teachers in Morarji Desai  


--- PAGE 298 ---
- 298 - 
 
 
WP No. 8442 of 2020 
AND CONNECTED MATTERS 
 
 
 
and Kittur Rani Chennamma residential 
Schools on contract basis into establishment 
of 
Karnataka 
Residential 
Educational 
Institution Society) (Special Regulations), 
2011 (for short ‘Absorption Regulations, 
2011’);  
ii) 
To 
quash 
the 
Karnataka 
Residential 
Education Society (Cadre and Recruitment) 
Regulations, 2011 (for short ‘Recruitment 
Regulations, 2011’) insofar as it relates to 
granting service weightage only to the 
principals and teachers appointed by the 
Karnataka 
Residential 
Educational 
Institutions Society (for short ‘Society’); 
iii) 
To direct the respondents to extend the 
benefit of service weightage as specified 
under the Recruitment Regulations, 2011 to 
the petitioners. 
 
iv) 
To quash the recruitment notification dated 
27-04-2011 inviting applications to fill the 
vacancies of principals and teachers; and  
 
v) 
To direct the respondents to continue the 
services of petitioners in the residential 
schools where they are now working and for 
other reliefs." 
The benefit of regularization that was granted to others in 
terms of Absorption Rules that were made were sought to be  


--- PAGE 299 ---
- 299 - 
 
 
WP No. 8442 of 2020 
AND CONNECTED MATTERS 
 
 
 
extended to the petitioners in those writ petitions and 
Regulations insofar as they relate to grant of service 
weightage only to Principals and teachers appointed by the 
Society was sought to be questioned and the benefit of 
service weightage as specified under the Recruitment 
Regulations of 2011 were sought to be extended and their 
continuance in service was an incidental prayer that was 
sought. 
This 
Court 
formulated 
certain 
points 
for 
consideration after hearing the petitioners therein. It is 
germane to notice them for considering the present issue as 
well. The points formulated are as follows: 
i) 
Whether the petitioners are entitled for 
absorption 
under 
the 
provisions 
of 
Absorption Regulations, 2011? 
 
ii) 
Whether the petitioners are entitled for 
service 
weightage 
as 
specified 
in 
the 
Recruitment Regulations, 2011? 
 
iii) 
Whether the recruitment notification dated 
27.04.2011 is liable to be quashed? 
 
 
 


--- PAGE 300 ---
- 300 - 
 
 
WP No. 8442 of 2020 
AND CONNECTED MATTERS 
 
 
The first point was whether the petitioners are entitled for 
absorption 
under 
the 
provisions 
of 
the 
Absorption 
Regulations, 2011. It was held that denial of benefit of 
absorption to the petitioners therein would not amount to 
discrimination or an action that would be arbitrary and 
declined to accept the said claim of the petitioners therein 
for absorption. The reasons rendered by the learned Single 
Judge on point No.1 is as follows: 
“On point No.(i) 
12. 
Learned Advocates for the petitioners 
have not advanced any arguments in so far as it 
relates to validity of Recruitment Regulations, 2011 
and the Absorption Regulations, 2011.  Therefore, it 
is not necessary for this court to go into the 
constitutional validity of these regulations.  Learned 
counsel for the petitioners contend that the 
principals and teachers appointed by the Society 
prior to 2004-2005 and continued in service till the 
date of coming into force of Absorption Regulations, 
2011 are entitled for absorption.  This benefit of 
absorption is denied to the petitioners firstly on the 
ground that they are not appointed by the Society 
and secondly on the ground that they are 
appointed by the Society and secondly on the 
ground that they are appointed subsequent to 
2004-2005 by Agency.  The material on record 
discloses that prior to 2000, the principals and 
teachers 
were 
appointed 
by 
Social 
Welfare 
Department, Backward class Department and the 
Education Department.  After the formation of the 
 
  


--- PAGE 301 ---
- 301 - 
 
 
WP No. 8442 of 2020 
AND CONNECTED MATTERS 
 
 
Society the residential schools were brought under 
its control from the year 2000.  The principals and 
teachers who were appointed prior to 2000 are 
continued under the society.  Subsequent to 2000, 
the Society appointed principals and teachers up to 
the year 2004-2005 and they are continued in 
services as on the date of Absorption Regulations, 
2011 came into force.  The respondents by issuing 
notifications, holding selection process and by 
following reservation and roster appointed the 
principals and teachers.  These teachers have put 
in service of 7 to 10  years and more.  Having 
regard to the age of these principals and teachers, 
length of service rendered by them and their 
appointment on merit and by following the 
reservation and roster, constitute a class by 
themselves.  In the circumstances, the Government 
after active consideration passed by impugned 
Absorption Regulations, 2011 only to such of the 
principals and teachers who are appointed prior to 
2004-2005 and continued in service upto the date 
of impugned regulations came into force in 2011 
subject to satisfying certain other conditions.  
Therefore, the principals and teachers appointed 
prior to 2004-2005 constitute a separate class. 
 
13. On the other hand the petitioners are 
all appointed subsequent to 2004-2005 through an 
Agency.  Though some of these petitioners are 
appointed pursuant to notifications, holding of 
selection 
process 
and 
following 
reservation, 
constitute by themselves a different class.  Further 
the petitioners are all appointed through Agency 
and their salaries are paid through agency.  Having 
regard to the nature of appointment length of 
service and their age, constitute by themselves a 
different class.  The Government by taking into 
consideration these relevant factors not extended 
the benefit of absorption to the principals and 
teachers appointed subsequent to 2004-2005. 
 
 


--- PAGE 302 ---
- 302 - 
 
 
WP No. 8442 of 2020 
AND CONNECTED MATTERS 
 
 
14. The Supreme Court in Shekawath 
Ali vs. State of Orissa (AIR 1955 SC 166) held 
that legislation enacted for the achievement 
of a particular job or purpose need not be of 
embracing.  It is for the Legislature to 
determine what categories it would embrace 
within the scope of legislation.  Merely 
because certain categories which would stand 
on the same footing as those which are 
covered by the legislation are left out would 
not render legislation which has been enacted 
in any manner discriminatory and violative of 
fundamental rights guaranteed by Article 14.  
Therefore the denial of benefit of absorption to the 
petitioners will not amount to discrimination, 
arbitrary and illegal.  Therefore point no.(i) is 
answered in negative.” 
 
10. The second point was whether the petitioners 
therein would be entitled to service weightage in terms of the 
Regulations. It was held that the petitioners therein were 
entitled to such weightage. The order of the learned Single 
Judge on point No.2 reads as follows: 
 “17. Admittedly, from 2004-05 onwards these 
residential schools are under the control of Zilla 
Panchayats 
in 
the 
respective 
Districts. 
The 
Government of Karnataka in their statement of 
objections filed before this Court specifically stated 
that to fill up the vacancies in the newly started 
schools subsequent to 2004-05 the Society requested 
the 
respective 
Deputy 
Commissioners, 
Zilla 
Panchayats, Social Welfare Department, Backward 
Class  Departments  to  engage  services  of  contract  
 


--- PAGE 303 ---
- 303 - 
 
 
WP No. 8442 of 2020 
AND CONNECTED MATTERS 
 
 
teachers 
by 
outsourcing 
on 
temporary 
basis. 
Accordingly, the petitioners are all appointed at the 
instance of third respondent Society. The appointment 
of the petitioners through agency is again as per the 
manual 
of 
residential 
schools 
issued 
by 
the 
Government of Karnataka through Social Welfare 
Department, the Standard tendered document issued 
by the Government of Karnataka and also the orders 
issued by the Society in some of the Districts, the 
selection of principals and teachers was made through 
Nehru Yuva Kendras and in some other Districts 
directly by Zilla Panchayats and in some other 
Districts through Agency. All these appointments are 
at the instance of the Society and as per the guidelines 
prescribed by it an the Government. Though the 
monthly salaries are paid through Agency the same is 
in terms of the Manual for residential schools and the 
Standard 
tendered 
document. 
Therefore, 
the 
appointment of petitioners was at the instance of 
the Society. In the circumstances, I decline to 
accept the contention of the respondents that in 
the matter of appointment of the petitioners 
through Agency the Society has no role to pay 
and there is no relationship of employer and 
employee. I hold that petitioners are appointed 
by the Society and on its instructions, through 
Agency. In the circumstances, the petitioners are 
entitled for the benefit of service weightage 
under the Recruitment Regulations, 2011.” 
 
 
 
 
 
 
(Emphasis supplied) 
While directing that the petitioners therein would be entitled 
to the benefit of weightage, it was observed that appointment 
of  petitioners  was  at the instance of the Society even if it is  


--- PAGE 304 ---
- 304 - 
 
 
WP No. 8442 of 2020 
AND CONNECTED MATTERS 
 
 
 
through an outsourced agency. The writ petitions were 
partly allowed holding that the petitioners were not entitled 
for absorption and were only entitled for the benefit of 
service weightage. The operative portion of the judgment 
rendered by the learned Single Judge reads as follows: 
“ORDER 
i) 
Writ petitions are partly allowed. 
 
ii) 
The Petitioners are not entitled for absorption 
under the Absorption Regulations, 2011. 
 
iii) 
The 
respondents 
shall 
regularise

iv)

v) 
The respondents to complete the selection 
process 
pursuant 
to 
the 
recruitment

and teachers. 
 
 


--- PAGE 305 ---
- 305 - 
 
 
WP No. 8442 of 2020 
AND CONNECTED MATTERS 
 
 
 
vi) 
Since respondents have already processed 
the applications and issued final selection 
list and additional selection list, they have to 
redo the process of selection by extending the

and similarly situated candidates. 
 
vii) 
Till the completion of selection process as 
stated above the present placement of 
petitioners and others who are continuing in

viii) 
The prayer in the writ petitions in so far as it

ix)

11. The afore-quoted order was called in question by 
the Society in Writ Appeal No. 5127 of 2012 and connected 
cases.  The Division Bench by its judgment dated              
28-02-2013 dismissed all the writ appeals. A Special Leave 
Petition preferred by the Society against both the aforesaid 
orders also came to be dismissed by the Apex Court in terms 
of its order dated 9-09-2013. Thus ended the claim of the 
petitioners for regularization of their services in terms of 
Absorption Rules as was done in the case of others.  


--- PAGE 306 ---
- 306 - 
 
 
WP No. 8442 of 2020 
AND CONNECTED MATTERS 
 
 
 
12. During the pendency of the aforesaid writ petitions, 
notwithstanding 
the 
existing 
Cadre 
and 
Recruitment 
Regulations, 
a 
notification 
was 
issued 
calling 
for 
applications from eligible candidates for teaching and non-
teaching posts on 27-04-2011 to be appointed through an 
outsourced agency. It appears that examination was 
conducted from 24-07-2011 to 27-07-2011 and several 
persons were appointed through the outsourced agency. 
These appointments were made during the pendency of the 
aforesaid writ petitions before this Court. In terms of 
directions by the learned single Judge or by the Division 
Bench the Society undertook the task of redoing the whole 
list in terms of the order and notified separate merit list by 
giving service weightage as was directed by this Court.  
 
13. After the said exercise, the Regulations were 
amended and grant of service weightage came to be deleted 
with effect from 07-08-2013. Therefore, from 7-08-2013 
onwards service weightage does not find a place in the Cadre  


--- PAGE 307 ---
- 307 - 
 
 
WP No. 8442 of 2020 
AND CONNECTED MATTERS 
 
 
 
and Recruitment Regulations. Most of the petitioners who 
were earlier before this Court in those cases are the 
petitioners in several of these cases. Service weightage was 
granted to most of the petitioners in the case at hand, but 
despite grant of such service weightage several of the 
petitioners could not even come up to cut off mark. 
Petitioners after having worked for several years and 
continuing to work, represented to the State Government for 
regularization of their services by grant of service weightage 
up to 2016. This request also came to be rejected and a 
direction was issued by the Government to relieve the 
petitioners appointed through outsourced Agencies and to 
recruit teachers by direct recruitment through the KPSC. 
This communication is issued by the Social Welfare 
Department to the Society on 04-04-2018. It is this order 
that is called in question by the petitioners in these cases.  
 
 
14. Learned Senior Counsel Sri V.Lakshminarayana 
representing  the  petitioners  in  all these  cases  would  


--- PAGE 308 ---
- 308 - 
 
 
WP No. 8442 of 2020 
AND CONNECTED MATTERS 
 
 
 
vehemently argue and contend that the petitioners are 
entitled to weightage up to the date on which Regulations 
were 
amended 
and 
weightage 
came 
to 
be 
deleted. 
Examinations were conducted in the year 2011, during the 
pendency of the writ petitions and adding weightage, though 
did not bring them on to the cut off mark between 2011 and 
2013, they had rendered service till the date of deletion of 
weightage. Therefore, those two orders will have to be 
considered and certain weightage will have to be given to the 
petitioners. He would further contend that the petitioners 
have continued to work from 2004-05 and are working even 
to this date and the petitioners have now rendered service 
ranging from 9 years to 16 years and would submit that the 
petitioners are entitled to regularization of their services as 
they have been teaching throughout.  
 
15. He would submit that the very concept of

WP No. 8442 of 2020

should not be made, more so, in the light of the Cadre and

months.  This according to learned Senior Counsel would 
amount to making the teachers work without payment of 
salary.   
 
16. On the other hand, learned Senior Counsel Prof. 
Ravivarma Kumar appearing for the Society, in all these

that  the  petitioners  are  not  paid  salary  as  no  teacher is  


--- PAGE 310 ---
- 310 - 
 
 
WP No. 8442 of 2020 
AND CONNECTED MATTERS 
 
 
 
made to work without payment of salary.  If they are 
working, they would be paid salary. 
 
 
17. I have given my anxious consideration to the 
submissions made by the respective learned Senior Counsel 
and have perused the material on record. 
 
 
18. The afore-narrated facts of establishment of 
Society, approach of the petitioners to this Court earlier and 
those proceedings as narrated hereinabove not being in 
dispute need not be reiterated. The issue in the case at hand 
is whether the petitioners are entitled to the benefit of 
weightage between the earlier examination and the date on 
which the Regulations of weightage came to be deleted and 
further, whether the petitioners are not entitled to 
regularization.  
 
19. The petitioners are all appointed on contract basis 
through  outsourced  agencies.   The nodal agency for  such  


--- PAGE 311 ---
- 311 - 
 
 
WP No. 8442 of 2020 
AND CONNECTED MATTERS 
 
 
appointments was the Society.  The claim of the petitioners 
that they are entitled to weightage between the date on 
which the initial examination took place till Regulations of 
weightage stood deleted, is unacceptable, as all these issues 
have been dealt with in the previous writ petitions, as 
quoted supra, filed by these very petitioners. The society, as 
against the order passed in the writ petitions, filed 
W.A.No.5127/2012 and connected cases wherein the 
Division Bench disposed of the said writ appeals by 
observing as follows: 
“21. We find no infirmity or irregularity in the 
order passed by the learned single Judge. The 
appellant has not made out a case to interfere with the 
order passed by the learned single Judge. In the 
result, the writ appeals filed by the appellant-Society 
are dismissed. 
 
In the result, the writ appeals filed by the 
appellant- Society are dismissed. We affirm the view 
taken 
by 
the 
learned 
single 
Judge 
that 
the 
respondents – Principals and Teachers shall be 
entitled for service weightage.  It is needless to 
mention that the respondent will be entitled for service 
weightage provided they have appeared for combined 
competitive examinations held on 24-07-2011 to 27-
07-2011. The appellant-Society as far as possible, try  
 


--- PAGE 312 ---
- 312 - 
 
 
WP No. 8442 of 2020 
AND CONNECTED MATTERS 
 
 
to accommodate the respondents, if, after giving 
service 
weightage 
they 
are 
selected, 
without 
disturbing the candidates who have already been 
selected and appointed. Since their appointments are 
made subject to result of the writ petitions, in the event 
of discontinuing any of them or withdrawing their 
appointments, for accommodating the respondents, 
they shall continue or withdraw the appointment “on 
last come first go” principle.” 
 
The Division Bench made it clear that the petitioners and 
the like would be entitled to service weightage provided they 
have appeared for the combined competitive examination 
held between 24-07-2011 and 27-07-2011 and the Society 
was directed to accommodate the respondents, if after giving 
service weightage they are selected without disturbing the 
candidates who have already been selected and appointed. 
Therefore, the service weightage was given to the petitioners 
and merit-list was redrawn. Even after grant of service 
weightage the petitioners did not qualify for such selection. 
It is therefore, service weightage cannot now be claimed by 
the petitioners for two years from 24-07-2011 up to date on 
which  the Regulations  were amended,  resulting in deletion  
 


--- PAGE 313 ---
- 313 - 
 
 
WP No. 8442 of 2020 
AND CONNECTED MATTERS 
 
 
of weightage, as the finding of the Division Bench, is binding 
upon the parties to the said lis and this Court as well.  
 
20. Insofar as the claim of regularization is concerned, 
the respondent-Society has placed on record certain orders 
of appointments appointing several of the petitioners in 
those petitions.  For the remainder of the petitioners who are 
not appointed, the learned Senior Counsel would again rely 
on the earlier judgment of the learned single Judge wherein, 
a specific point was formulated for consideration, whether 
the petitioners were entitled to absorption and the 
absorption of others was discriminatory.  This Court (supra) 
while declining to answer the said point in favour of the 
petitioners had clearly held that having regard to the nature 
of appointments, length of service and their age, they 
constitute a different class and the Government did not 
extend the benefit of absorption of principals and other 
teachers appointed subsequent to 2004 and 2005  and  held  
 


--- PAGE 314 ---
- 314 - 
 
 
WP No. 8442 of 2020 
AND CONNECTED MATTERS 
 
 
 
that it is for the legislature to determine what categories it 
would embrace within the scope of legislation.  
 
21. The claim for regularization having been turned 
down earlier, would not, in my considered view, be an 
impediment to consider their cases, if the petitioners are so 
entitled to, in terms of law as laid down by the Apex Court in 
plethora of judgments in the aftermath of STATE OF 
KARNATAKA v. UMADEVI - (2006) 4 SCC 1 as the 
submission is that the petitioners have now put in 9 to 16 
years of service, albeit, it being refuted by the learned Senior 
Counsel representing the Society.   
 
22.  The judgment in the case of UMADEVI was 
rendered on 10-04-2006. By then, the petitioners in the case 
at hand were all appointed albeit through outsourced 
agencies. Much water has flown after the judgment of the 
Apex Court in the case of UMADEVI. The subsequent 
judgments  that  are  rendered  would  clearly  indicate  that  


--- PAGE 315 ---
- 315 - 
 
 
WP No. 8442 of 2020 
AND CONNECTED MATTERS 
 
 
 
employees who are appointed on  contract basis were 
entitled to be considered for regularization, if they have 
worked uninterruptedly for 10 years and the appointment 
not being litigious. Therefore, it is for the State Government 
to decide the cases of the petitioners exercising its 
discretion, if the petitioners are entitled for regularization in 
the light of subsequent judgments rendered in the aftermath 
of UMADEVI.   
 
 
 
23. Insofar as it pertains to the submissions of the 
learned Senior Counsel that the petitioners are not paid 
salary despite them working, the petitioners would be at 
liberty to give such representations to the respondents, 
giving out details of their work performed and on verification 
of the records, it is for the respondents, the Society or the 
State to consider those representations in accordance with 
law, as, if the petitioners are made to work without payment 
of   salary,  as  is  alleged,  it  would amount to begar  and  


--- PAGE 316 ---
- 316 - 
 
 
WP No. 8442 of 2020 
AND CONNECTED MATTERS 
 
 
 
violative of Article 23 of the Constitution of India.  Therefore, 
the State shall consider those grievances of the petitioners. 
 
 
24. It is germane, albeit, as a parting observation to 
notice that in the teeth of existence of the Cadre and 
Recruitment 
Regulations, 
the 
Society 
resorted 
to 
outsourcing. Outsource agencies have mushroomed into 
mountainous proportions in the last 10 to 15 years. It is 
because of the policy of the State to get appointments made 
through outsource agencies.  Since it is in the realm of 
policy, 
this 
Court 
would 
not 
pronounce 
upon 
its 
permissibility or otherwise, but what needs to be noticed 
with concern is the fact that the appointments to the post of 
teachers are also being made through outsourced agency by, 
calling for tenders.  If a teacher from out of his monthly 
salary has to pay, month on month, a portion of his salary to 
the outsourced agency, under the head of service charges. 
There cannot be any better exploitation of human labour by 
the State, by encouraging appointments of teachers through  


--- PAGE 317 ---
- 317 - 
 
 
WP No. 8442 of 2020 
AND CONNECTED MATTERS 
 
 
outsourced agencies.  In the life of a student, teacher plays a 
very important role.  A teacher is a dynamic force; the lamp 
of knowledge; a harbinger who would mould, hone and 
chisel the life and living of a child.  It is for this reason a 
teacher would need security of tenure and not suffer 
vagrancies with regard to his work or wages, as they need to 
be dynamic, resurrect themselves to adopt to the changing 
teaching skills.  Reference being made to the words of 
Dr.APJ Abdul Kalam, in the circumstances is apposite: 
 
“The child goes to school to learn; 
 
 Learning leads to creativity; 
 
 Creativity leads to intellectuality; 
 
 Intellectuality makes the child a great person.” 
 
 
25. The afore-quoted parting observation is vindicated 
by the submissions and documents placed before the Court 
during the submissions.  Notification dated 16-05-2013 
depicts that Principals and other teachers are appointed on 
contract 
basis 
through 
outsourced 
agencies. 
 
The 
Notification reads as follows:

posts have been notified and the final select list is also out 
as is indicated by the KPSC.  Therefore, the State shall 
endeavour to continue its effort of recruitment of teachers in 
terms of the Cadre and Recruitment Rules and not resort to 
appointment on contract basis through outsourced agencies 
by a process of inviting tender.   
 
27. Insofar as the issue of the regularization of the 
petitioners as is sought, it is for the State to take a decision 
on exercise of its discretion with regard to the service of the 
petitioners in the event they are continuing to work till the 
notification came to be issued on 27-5-2017. No mandamus 
can be issued to the Society or the State to consider the 
cases of the petitioners for regularization of their services, as 
it involves examination of manifold facts, documents and 
service particulars of the petitioners.  What the petitioners 
are entitled to, is only an observation for exercise of such 
discretion by the State.

28. For the aforesaid reasons, I pass the following: 
O R D E R 
(i) 
Writ Petitions are disposed. 
  
(ii) 
The claim of the petitioners for grant of weightage 
between 2011 and 2013 till the date on which 
Regulations with regard to weightage stood 
deleted stands dismissed.  
 
(iii) 
The petitioners are not entitled to issuance of any 
mandamus for regularization of their services.  
(iv) 
It would be open to the State to consider their 
cases/grievances 
bearing 
in 
mind 
the 
observations made in the course of this order, in 
the event they would satisfy all the parameters 
stipulated in the judgment rendered in the case 
of UMADEVI and its aftermath. 
 
 
Ordered accordingly. 
 
 
In view of disposal of the petitions, all pending 
applications also stand disposed.
